Here, the second summand clearly converges to zero, whereas this follows for the first one, since
\sup_{s\leq 1}|X_{s}^{\rho_{n},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}}-X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta}|\rightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0
by Theorem
1.5
in
Le Gall 1984
. By Lemma
18.12
in
Kallenberg 2021
, the convergence in probability of the stochastic integrals follows once we establish the convergence
\langle X^{\rho_{n},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}}\rangle_{1}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}\langle X^{\rho,\alpha,\beta}\rangle_{1}
of the respective quadratic variations. By definition, we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle X^{\rho_{n},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}}\rangle_{1}-\langle X^{\rho,\alpha,\beta}\rangle_{1}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{1}\sigma_{\rho_{n},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}}^{2}(X_{s}^{\rho_{n},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}})ds-\int_{0}^{1}\sigma_{\rho,\alpha,\beta}^{2}(X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})ds\right|

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{n})}(X_{s}^{\rho_{n},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}})-\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho)}(X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})\right|ds

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\beta^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\mathbbm{1}_{[\rho_{n},\infty)}(X_{s}^{\rho_{n},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}})-\mathbbm{1}_{[\rho,\infty)}(X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})\right|ds

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\left|\alpha_{n}^{2}-\alpha^{2}\right|+\left|\beta_{n}^{2}-\beta^{2}\right|.

Clearly, both summands in the last line converge to zero. We now prove this for the first summand on the right-hand side, the second one can be dealt with analogously. To this aim, let
\epsilon>0
and
n
large enough such that for
A_{n}:=\{\sup_{s\leq 1}|X_{s}^{\rho_{n},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}}-X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta}|\leq\epsilon\}
we have
\mathbb{P}(A_{n})\geq 1-\epsilon
. On this set, we have

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{n})}(X_{s}^{\rho_{n},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}})-\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho)}(X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})\right|ds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{n})}(X_{s}^{\rho_{n},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}})-\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{n})}(X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})\right|ds

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{n})}(X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})-\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho)}(X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})\right|ds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathbbm{1}_{[\rho_{n}-\epsilon,\rho_{n}+\epsilon]}(X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})ds+\int_{0}^{1}\left|\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho_{n})}(X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})-\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,\rho)}(X_{s}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})\right|ds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{1}{\min\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}}\left(2\epsilon+|\rho_{n}-\rho|\right)\sup_{\rho-|\rho-\rho_{n}|\leq y\leq\rho+|\rho-\rho_{n}|}L_{1}^{y}(X^{\rho,\alpha,\beta}).

Now, the claim follows by tightness of
\sup_{\rho-|\rho-\rho_{n}|\leq y\leq\rho+|\rho-\rho_{n}|}L_{1}^{y}(X^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})
, which in turn is a consequence of the fact that
L_{1}^{\bullet}(X^{\rho,\alpha,\beta})
is continuous by Corollary
1.8
in
Revuz and Yor 1999
and thus attains its maximum and minimum on compact sets.
∎

7
Proof of Proposition
3.3

For the proof of Proposition
3.3
recall the normalized log-likelihood function
\ell_{n,\gamma}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})
from (
3.6
) and its decomposition (
3.8
) into two parts, where the first one equals
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta_{\rho})
given in (
3.1
) and only depends on
\theta_{\rho}
. Let
\epsilon>0
, the set
E^{\gamma}
be defined as

\displaystyle E^{\gamma}=\begin{cases}\{L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(X)>0\},&\textrm{ if }\gamma=1,\\
\Omega,&\textrm{ else},\end{cases}

(7.1)

and
B_{n}
be an event with
\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(B_{n})\geq 1-\epsilon
for
n
large enough that will be specified later and consists of paths with typical behavior. In the rest of this section, we tacitly assume
(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})\in\mathbb{R}\times(-\alpha_{0},\infty)\times(-\beta_{0},\infty)
. The statement of Proposition
3.3
will follow once we show that

\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left(n^{(1+\gamma)/2}|\hat{\rho}_{n}-\rho_{0}|>K_{1},\sqrt{n}\max\{|\hat{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{0}|,|\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0}|\}>K_{2},E^{\gamma}\right)

can be bounded by a multiple of
\epsilon
for
K_{1},K_{2}
large enough. As
\ell_{n,\gamma}(0,0,0)=0
, this probability is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left(\sup_{n^{(1+\gamma)/2}|\theta_{\rho}|>K_{1},\sqrt{n}\max\{|\theta_{\alpha}|,|\theta_{\beta}|\}>K_{2}}\ell_{n,\gamma}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})\geq 0,B_{n}\cap E^{\gamma}\right)+\epsilon

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sum_{j=1}^{3}\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left(\sup_{(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})\in\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{j}(K)}\left(\ell_{n,\gamma}^{1}(\theta_{\rho})+\ell_{n,\gamma}^{2}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})\right)\geq 0,B_{n}\cap E^{\gamma}\right)+\epsilon,

where
K=(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3})\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{3}
and the three index sets for the parameter vector
(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})
are given as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{1}(K)

\displaystyle:=\left\{(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta}):\max\{|\theta_{\alpha}|,|\theta_{\beta}|\}\leq K_{2}/\sqrt{n},K_{1}n^{-(1+\gamma)/2}<|\theta_{\rho}|<K_{3}n^{-\gamma/2}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{2}(K)

\displaystyle:=\left\{(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta}):\max\{|\theta_{\alpha}|,|\theta_{\beta}|\}>K_{2}/\sqrt{n},|\theta_{\rho}|\leq K_{3}n^{-\gamma/2}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{3}(K)

\displaystyle:=\left\{(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta}):\theta_{\rho}>K_{3}n^{-\gamma/2}\right\}.

In Subsection
F.1
in Appendix
F
we then prove that
K_{3}
can be chosen large enough such that the corresponding probability is bounded by
\epsilon
, then for this
K_{3}
we construct the corresponding
K_{2}(K_{3})
to bound the probability with
\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{2}(K)
by
\epsilon
and finally for given values
K_{2},K_{3}
we choose
K_{1}(K_{2},K_{3})
appropriately, i.e. the strategy is

\displaystyle\textrm{choose }K_{3}\ \longrightarrow\textrm{ choose }K_{2}=K_{2}(K_{3})\ \longrightarrow\textrm{ choose }K_{1}=K_{1}(K_{2},K_{3}).

As can be expected by the definition of these sets, the term
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{1}
will dominate on the set
\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{1}
and
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{2}
turns out to be negligible on this set. On
\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{2}
this swaps and
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{2}
dominates
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{1}
. On the remaining set
\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{3}
, however, there exists a subtle interplay of both of them that is described later. A crucial step to formally establish the aforementioned claims is to split both likelihood functions into a dominating term that can be understood reasonably well and a remainder (the form of this decomposition varies with
\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{j}
). Similar to the consistency proof under the assumption of known
\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}
this is done on a set ensuring typical behavior of the paths of
X
. Note that this reasoning applies both for the high-frequency scheme
\gamma=1
and all intermediate regimes
\gamma\in(0,1)
. In particular, we set

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{n}&=\left\{-\Gamma\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\underline{X}_{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},\overline{X}_{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\Gamma\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{|t-s|<\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}|X_{t}-X_{s}|\leq(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9}\right\}\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\cap\left\{\inf_{y\in[\rho_{0}-\zeta\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},\rho_{0}+\zeta\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}]}L_{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{y}(X)>\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\xi\right\},\end{split}

(7.2)

where the constants
\Gamma,\xi,\zeta
are chosen such that
\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(A_{n})>1-\epsilon
for
n\geq n_{0}
large enough. This is possible for
\Gamma
by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality and follows for
\xi,\zeta
by Lemma
5.1
, whereas it is a consequence of Theorem
1
in
Fischer and Nappo 2010
for the second set in this intersection. On the set
A_{n}
we have the following result that allows to estimate the number of
X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
falling into a given interval.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
-\zeta\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq a,b\leq\zeta\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
such that
\rho_{0}-a\leq\rho_{0}+b
and
a+b>3(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9}
. Then,

\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}-a<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}+b\}}\geq\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\frac{\xi(a+b)}{\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}

As
X_{s}<\rho_{0}-(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9}
for some
s\in[(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma},k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}]
implies
X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}
on
A_{n}
, we obtain using the occupation times formula

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}-a<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}+b\}}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\frac{1}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\int_{0}^{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\mathbbm{1}_{(\rho_{0}-a+(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9},\rho_{0}+b-(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9}]}(X_{s})ds

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\frac{1}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\int_{\rho_{0}-a+(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9}}^{\rho_{0}+b-(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9}}L_{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{y}(X)dy

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\frac{1}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\xi\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}(a+b-2(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9})

and the claim follows by the assumption
a+b>3(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9}
.
∎

In what follows, we describe the decomposition of both
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{1}
and
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{2}
into leading terms and remainders. To this aim, let

\displaystyle\Theta_{n,\gamma}^{1}=\left[0,n^{1/4-\gamma/2}\right]\quad\textrm{ and }\quad\Theta_{n,\gamma}^{2}=\left(n^{1/4-\gamma/2},\infty\right).

(7.3)

According to straightforward modification of Lemma
D.2
for
\gamma\in(0,1)
and Lemma
4.1
in
Brutsche and Rohde 2026
for
\gamma=1
(see Remark
F.1
), there exists a sequence
(B_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
A_{n}\subset B_{n}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(B_{n}^{c})<\epsilon
for which we have

\displaystyle\ell_{n,\gamma}^{1}(\theta_{\rho})\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}=\left[\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}-1\right)\right]\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}\}}+R_{n,\gamma}^{1}(\theta_{\rho}),

(7.4)

where

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta_{\rho}\in\Theta_{n,\gamma}^{j}}\left|R_{n,\gamma}^{1}(\theta_{\rho})\right]\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}\leq C_{1}n^{1/2+\gamma(1-j)/6}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}.

(7.5)

For
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{2}
, we introduce the decomposition

\displaystyle\ell_{n,\gamma}^{2}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})=H_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})+H_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})+R_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})+R_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta}),

(7.6)

where the leading terms
H_{n,\gamma}^{\leq},H_{n,\gamma}^{>}
are given as

\displaystyle H_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left[\log\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{(\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha})^{2}}-1\right)\frac{(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}\}}

\displaystyle H_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left[\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}}{\beta_{0}+\theta_{\beta}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{(\beta_{0}+\theta_{\beta})^{2}}-1\right)\frac{(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{\beta_{0}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}>\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}\}}

and the remainder terms
R_{n,\gamma}^{\leq},R_{n,\gamma}^{>}
as

\displaystyle R_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\ell_{n}^{2}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})-H_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}}\}}

\displaystyle R_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\ell_{n}^{2}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})-H_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}>\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}}\}}.

Heuristically, for the squared increments we have
(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}\approx\alpha_{0}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}
in case
X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\ll\rho_{0}
and analogously
(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}\approx\beta_{0}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}
for observations
X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\gg\rho_{0}
. Hence, for
\theta_{\rho}\geq 0
, the terms
H_{n}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})
and
H_{n}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})
will often be substituted with their counterparts

\displaystyle\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})

\displaystyle:=\left[\log\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{(\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha})^{2}}-1\right)\right]\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\left[\log\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{(\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha})^{2}}-1\right)\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}\right]\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}\}}

\displaystyle\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})

\displaystyle:=\left[\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}}{\beta_{0}+\theta_{\beta}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{(\beta_{0}+\theta_{\beta})^{2}}-1\right)\right]\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}>\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}\}}.

Note that the first and third square bracket are non-positive and equal to zero if and only if
\theta_{\alpha}=0
and
\theta_{\beta}=0
, respectively. Moreover, a second order Taylor expansion of the function
f(x)=\log(x)-(x^{2}-1)/2
gives
f(x)=-(1-\xi^{-2})(x-1)^{2}
for some intermediate value
\xi
between
1
and
x
. In case the sums counting the cases where
X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}
and that where
X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}>\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}
are both of order
n
, this means that both leading terms are of constant order for
\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta}=K_{2}/\sqrt{n}
and negative. For
\theta_{\rho}
not being too small, this order
n
is given for both summands by Lemma
7.1
and the number of
k
with
\rho_{0}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}
turns out to be small, which together is the reason why
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{2}
is the dominant term on the set
\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{2}
. Note in particular, that a negative contribution emerges if either
|\theta_{\alpha}|>K_{2}/\sqrt{n}
or
|\theta_{\beta}|>K_{2}/\sqrt{n}
. To formally give the proof in Appendix
F.1
, the next result will be used that specifies the error when switching by the different quantities as outlined above. Its proof is deferred to Appendix
F.2
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\epsilon,L>0
, and
g(x)=|\log(x)|+\frac{1}{2}|x^{2}-1|
. Then there exists
n_{0}=n_{0}(L)\in\mathbb{N}
, a constant
C=C(\epsilon,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},L,\Gamma,\xi,\zeta)>0
and a sequence of sets
(B_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
B_{n}\subset A_{n}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(B_{n}^{c})<\epsilon
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
:

(a)

For small values of
\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta}
we have the uniform estimates

\displaystyle\sup_{|\theta_{\alpha}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\sup_{0\leq\theta_{\rho}\leq Ln^{-\gamma/2}}\left|H_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})\right|\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}\leq CL\quad\textrm{ and }

\displaystyle\sup_{|\theta_{\beta}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\sup_{0\leq\theta_{\rho}\leq Ln^{-\gamma/2}}\left|H_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})\right|\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}\leq CL.

(b)

For small values of
\theta_{\rho}
, we have the uniform estimates

\displaystyle\sup_{|\theta_{\rho}|\leq Ln^{-\gamma/2}}\left|H_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})-H_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(0,\theta_{\alpha})\right|\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}\leq CL\sqrt{n}g\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha}}\right)\quad\textrm{ and }

\displaystyle\sup_{|\theta_{\rho}|\leq Ln^{-\gamma/2}}\left|H_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})-H_{n,\gamma}^{>}(0,\theta_{\beta})\right|\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}\leq CL\sqrt{n}g\left(\frac{\beta_{0}}{\beta_{0}+\theta_{\beta}}\right).

(c)

For
\theta_{\rho}\geq 0
we have the difference estimates

\displaystyle\left|H_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})-\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})\right|\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}\leq C\sqrt{n}g\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha}}\right)\quad\textrm{ and }

\displaystyle\left|H_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})-\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})\right|\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}\leq C\sqrt{n}g\left(\frac{\beta_{0}}{\beta_{0}+\theta_{\beta}}\right).

(d)

Let
R>0
and
d_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}:=\log(2)-\log(1-|\alpha_{0}-\beta_{0}|/(\alpha_{0}+\beta_{0}))
. For
R_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}
,
R_{n,\gamma}^{>}
we find function
T_{n,\gamma}^{\leq},T_{n,\gamma}^{>}
and a constant
C(R)>0
such that for
\star\in\{\leq,>\}
and
n\geq n_{0}(R)
,

\displaystyle R_{n,\gamma}^{\star}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})

\displaystyle\leq C(R)\left(g\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha}}\right)+g\left(\frac{\beta_{0}}{\beta_{0}+\theta_{\beta}}\right)\right)T_{n,\gamma}^{\star}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+nd_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\theta_{\alpha}>R\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\theta_{\beta}>R\}}\right),

where the functions
T_{n,\gamma}^{\leq},T_{n,\gamma}^{>}
satisfy the bound

\sup_{\theta_{\rho}\in\Theta_{n,\gamma}^{j}}\sup_{\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta}}\left|T_{n,\gamma}^{\star}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})\right|\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}\leq Cn^{1/2+\gamma(1-j)/6}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{n}}.

It remains to deal with the set
\mathcal{T}_{n,\gamma}^{3}
, where both
\theta_{\rho}
and
\max\{|\theta_{\alpha}|,\theta_{\beta}|\}
deviate from zero. Here, the reasoning outlined above does not apply and longer for two different reasons:

(P1)

The second summand in
\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}
is not necessarily negligible any longer as for large values of
\theta_{\rho}
the number of
k
for which
\rho_{0}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}
is also large and the factor in front is not necessarily negative.

(P2)

In case
\theta_{\alpha}\approx 0
, we automatically have
|\theta_{\beta}|>K_{2}/\sqrt{n}
and therefore the square bracket in the definition of
\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{>}
is negative. However, for large values of
\theta_{\rho}
, there might be few
k
with
X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}>\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}
.

Nevertheless, for large
\theta_{\rho}
, also
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{1}
is quite negative and we try to balance this out with the above mentioned effects of
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{2}
. To overcome these issues, we combine the expansions (
7.4
) and (
7.6
) to

\displaystyle\ell_{n,\gamma}^{1}(\theta_{\rho})+\ell_{n,\gamma}^{2}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})

\displaystyle=\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(0,\theta_{\alpha})+\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})+T_{n,\gamma}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})+R_{n,\gamma}^{all}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta}),

(7.7)

where

\displaystyle T_{n,\gamma}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})-\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(0,\theta_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\left[\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}-1\right)\right]\sup_{\theta_{\rho}\in\Theta_{n,\gamma}^{j}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}\}}

\displaystyle=\left[\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}-1\right)+\log\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{(\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha})^{2}}-1\right)\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}\}}

and

\displaystyle R_{n,\gamma}^{all}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})

\displaystyle=R_{n,\gamma}^{1}(\theta_{\rho})+R_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})+R_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta})

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt+\left(H_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})-\overline{H}^{\leq}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\alpha})\right)+\left(H_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})-\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{>}(\theta_{\rho},\theta_{\beta})\right).

As discussed earlier, both
\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}
and
\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{>}
are non-positive. Surprisingly, this also turns out to be true for the term
T_{n,\gamma}
that combines terms of
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{1}
and
\ell_{n,\gamma}^{2}
, see Lemma
F.2
in Appendix
F.3
that establishes

\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}-1\right)+\log\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{(\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha})^{2}}-1\right)\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}\leq 0

with equality in case
\theta_{\alpha}=\beta_{0}-\alpha_{0}
. The crucial observation is now that either
\overline{H}_{n,\gamma}^{\leq}
is close to zero (which happens for
\theta_{\alpha}\approx 0
), in which case
T_{n,\gamma}
is significantly negative or the other way around. All details for these heuristics are fully provided in Appendix
F.1
. When working these out, the only remaining obstacle is that balancing the leading terms with the remainders is difficult for
\theta_{\beta}\to-\beta_{0}
or
\theta_{\beta}\to\infty
and at the same time
\theta_{\rho}
being very large (i.e. close to
\sup_{t\leq n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}X_{t}
). In this case the bound provided by Lemma
7.2
(d) is no longer useful as
g(\beta_{0}/(\beta_{0}+\theta_{\beta}))
explodes. However, the problem occurs only for rare events in the sense of atypical path behavior and the probability of these can be controlled reasonably well. This is formally established by substituting the decomposition (
7.7
) with that given in Lemma
F.4
. The crucial step in its proof is to construct an injective mapping

\displaystyle\left\{k:X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}\leq X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\longrightarrow\left\{k:X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right\}\cup\left\{k:X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\geq\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}>\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}\right\},

which turns out to be possible for all observations
X_{0},X_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},\dots,X_{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
for which
X_{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
is bounded awaw from the global maximum of the path
(X_{t})_{0\leq t\leq n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
. This event is seen to have high probability by means of the Dambis–Dubins–Schwarz theorem. All details are provided in Subsection
F.3
.

This supplementary material is organized as follows:

Contents

A
Notation

Thoughout the whole technical supplement,
C_{\alpha,\beta}
denotes some real and positive constant that only depends on
\alpha
and
\beta
but may change from line to line. Any other dependencies are highlighed explicitly, for example by writing
C_{\alpha,\beta}(K)
in case the constant additionally depends on
K
. Sometimes we want to highlight that the constant
C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}
only depends on one of the parameters in which case the other one is omitted.
We frequently use the notation

\mathbb{P}(A,B):=\mathbb{P}(A\cap B).

We denote
\mathbb{P}_{0}=\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}
, where the subscript
(\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0})
indicates that
X
solves (
1.1
) with
\sigma_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}
and arbitrary starting point
x_{0}
. In cases where an argument involves two solutions of (
1.1
) with different starting points, this is explicitly highlighted by writing
X^{x_{0}}
. In all other cases, an arbitrary starting point is tacitly assumed.

B
Some preliminary results on OBM

By a straightforward case-by-case study, one obtains that the transition density (
2.1
) is dominated by a Gaussian density, i.e.

\displaystyle p_{t}^{\rho,\alpha,\beta}(x,y)\leq\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi t}}\frac{\max\{\alpha,\beta\}}{\min\{\alpha,\beta\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-x)^{2}}{2t\max\{\alpha^{2},\beta^{2}\}}\right).

(B.1)

From this, one can easily deduce the following result for an OBM solving (
1.1
) with
\sigma_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}
and
X_{0}=x_{0}
.

Corollary B.1
.

Let
a\in\mathbb{R}
,
b\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and
0\leq l<k
. Then we have

\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\left.\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-a)^{2}}{2b\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)\right|X_{l\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\leq c_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(b)\frac{1}{\sqrt{k-l}}

for some constant
c_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(b)>0
not depending on
n
and
a
.

Corollary
B.1
is often used in combination with the inequalities

\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq 2\sqrt{n},\quad\textrm{ and }\quad\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k(k-l)}}\leq 4n.

Both of them are easily obtained by comparison with corresponding integral and applied without further notice.

Corollary B.2
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists a constant
C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(p)
such that

\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\left.\frac{|X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}|^{p}}{(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{p/2}}\right|X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(p).

Using the bound (
B.1
) directly reveals

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\left.\frac{|X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}|^{p}}{(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{p/2}}\right|X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|y-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}|^{p}}{(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{p/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)dy

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|y|^{p}\exp\left(-\frac{y^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}}\right)dy

and the integral is bounded by a constant depending on
p
.
∎

C
Proof of (1A) for
\gamma\in(0,1)
and minimax lower bound

The proof of (1A) for
\gamma\in(0,1)
will be based on the decomposition

\displaystyle\ell_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)=B_{n,,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)+M_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta),

(C.1)

of the sequential version

\ell_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)=\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil nt\rceil}\log\left(\frac{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+\theta,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\right)

of the log-likelihood in (
3.1
), where

B_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta):=\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil nt\rceil}\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\left.\log\left(\frac{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+\theta,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\right)\right|X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]

and
M_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)=\ell_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)-B_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)
. This decomposition is useful, as
M_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)
is now a sum of martingale increments. Moreover, we have the following expansion of the drift term
B_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}
.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
|\theta|\leq K\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
and
X=(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
an OBM started in
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
with diffusion coefficient
\sigma_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}
. Then we have

B_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)=-|\theta|\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\{\theta\geq 0\}}F_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\theta<0\}}\tilde{F}_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\right)\Lambda_{n,\gamma}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left((X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})_{k=0,\dots,\lceil nt\rceil}\right)+r_{n,\gamma}(t,\theta),

where

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n,\gamma}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left((X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})_{k=0,\dots,\lceil nt\rceil}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil nt\rceil}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\alpha_{0}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil nt\rceil}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\geq\rho_{0}\}}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\beta_{0}^{2}}\right).

F_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}=-\left(\frac{2(\alpha_{0}-\beta_{0})}{\alpha_{0}\beta_{0}}+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\beta_{0}}\frac{2}{\alpha_{0}+\beta_{0}}\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}\right)\right),\qquad\tilde{F}_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}=F_{\beta_{0},\alpha_{0}}

and

\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\sup_{|\theta^{\prime}|\leq|\theta|}\sup_{s\leq t}|r_{n,\gamma}(s,\theta^{\prime})|\right]\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(K)\frac{\theta^{2}\sqrt{n}}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\sqrt{t}

for some constant
C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(K)>0
that is independent of
n
and
\theta
.

The proof is completely analogous to that of Proposition
2.1
in
Brutsche and Rohde 2026
with the generalized step size
\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}
instead of
1/n
.
∎

Secondly, we need a bound on the second moment of the (modified) summands of
\ell_{n,\gamma,t}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}
. To this aim we define

\displaystyle Z_{k}^{j}(\theta^{\prime},\theta):=\log\left(\frac{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+\theta,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{I_{j,k}^{\theta^{\prime},\theta}}

(C.2)

for
j\in\{1,3,4,5,6,7,9\}
, whereas

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z_{k}^{2}(\theta^{\prime},\theta)&:=\left[\log\left(\frac{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+\theta,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}\right)-\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}\right)\right]\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{\theta^{\prime},\theta}},\\
Z_{k}^{8}(\theta^{\prime},\theta)&:=\left[\log\left(\frac{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+\theta,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+\theta^{\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}\right)-\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}\right)\right]\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{\theta^{\prime},\theta}}.\end{split}

(C.3)

where the indicator sets are given in (
5.5
). For these
Z_{k}^{j}
, we have the following result.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
K>0
,
-K\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\theta^{\prime}<\theta\leq K\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
and
X=(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
an OBM started in
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
with diffusion coefficient
\sigma_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}
. Then for every
j=1,\dots,9
and
1\leq k\leq n
,

\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\left(Z_{k}^{j}(\theta^{\prime},\theta)\right)^{2}\right]\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}|\theta^{\prime}-\theta|^{2}\frac{1}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\sqrt{k}}.

The proof is analogous to that of Proposition
2.4
in
Brutsche and Rohde 2026
. In each step, the stepsize
1/n
of this article has to be replaced by
\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}
, yielding the desired result.
∎

With Lemma
C.1
and
C.2
we are now in a position to give the missing proof of (1A) in the intermediate regime
\gamma\in(0,1)
.

Recall that the coupling time
T_{\rho_{0}}^{x_{0}}
given in (
3.4
) is independent of
n
with
\mathbb{P}(T_{\rho_{0}}^{x_{0}}<\infty)=1
. Hence, for any
\epsilon>0
we can choose
T_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathbb{P}(T_{\rho_{0}}^{x_{0}}\leq T_{0})>1-\epsilon
. It then remains to prove that for
\gamma\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in[-K,K]}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\log\left(\frac{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+zn^{-(1+\gamma)/2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\overline{X}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c},\overline{X}_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c})}{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\overline{X}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c},\overline{X}_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c})}\right)\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0.

By using exactly the same reasoning, the same holds true for
\overline{X}^{c}
replaced by
X^{\rho_{0}}
. For the proof, we use the drift and martingale decomposition (
C.1
)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\log\left(\frac{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+zn^{-(1+\gamma)/2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\overline{X}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c},\overline{X}_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c})}{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\overline{X}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c},\overline{X}_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c})}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=B_{n,\gamma,(T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}+1)/n)}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left(zn^{-(1+\gamma)/2}\right)+M_{n,\gamma,(T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}+1)/n}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left(zn^{-(1+\gamma)/2}\right)

and prove that the supremum over
z\in[-K,K]
of both summands converges to zero in probability.

Drift part.
By Lemma
C.1
we directly obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in[-K,K]}\left|B_{n,\gamma,(T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}+1)/n}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left(zn^{-(1+\gamma)/2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}Kn^{-(1+\gamma)/2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\exp\left(-\frac{(\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\sup_{z\in[-K,K]}\left|r_{n,\gamma}\left((T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}+1)/n,zn^{-(1+\gamma)/2}\right)\right|.

Taking expectations on both side gives with Lemma
C.1
and Corollary
B.1

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z\in[-K,K]}\left|B_{n,\gamma,(T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}+1)/n}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left(zn^{-(1+\gamma)/2}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha,\beta}Kn^{-(1+\gamma)/2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}+C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\frac{K^{2}n^{-(1+\gamma)}}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\sqrt{\frac{T_{0}}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}+1}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(T_{0},K)\left(n^{-(1-\gamma)/2}+n^{-1+\gamma/2}\right)\longrightarrow 0.

Martingale part.
Here, we could split the martingale part
M_{n,t}
into nine different terms according to the nine regimes
I_{j,k}^{0,\theta}
given in (
5.5
). Lemma
C.2
entails that the second moments of individual summands is then different for
j=1,3,4,5,6,7,9
and
j=2,8
. For this reason, we split
M_{n,\gamma,t}
into two parts, namely

M_{n,\gamma,(T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}+1)/n}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})=M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z)+M_{n,\gamma}^{2}(z),

where both
M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z)
and
M_{n,\gamma}^{2}(z)
are sums of martingale differences and given as

\displaystyle M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{9}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\left(Z_{k}^{j}(0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})-\mathbb{E}\left[\left.Z_{k}^{j}(0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})\right|\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\right)

and

\displaystyle M_{n,\gamma}^{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}\right|\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\right),

where
Z_{k}^{j}
are given in (
C.2
) and (
C.3
) and
I_{2,k}^{0,\theta}
and
I_{8,k}^{0,\theta}
are defined in (
5.5
). In what follows, we will prove
\sup_{0\leq z\leq K}|M_{n}^{i}(z)|\rightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0
for
i=1,2
.

\bullet\ \mathbf{M_{n,\gamma}^{1}}
.

We show stochastic convergence of
\sup_{z\in[0,K]}|M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z)|
to zero by proving

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z\in[0,K]}|M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z)|\right]\longrightarrow 0,

which is done by chaining and variance bounds. By the special structure of the terms
Z_{k}^{j}
we have for
0\leq z^{\prime}\leq z\leq K

\displaystyle M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z)-M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{9}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\left(Z_{k}^{j}(z^{\prime}\psi_{n},z\psi_{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[\left.Z_{k}^{j}(z^{\prime}\psi_{n},z\psi_{n})\right|\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\right).

Because the indicators for
Z_{k}^{j},Z_{k}^{l}
are disjoint for
j\neq l
and the difference
M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z)-M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z^{\prime})
is a sum of martingale increments, iterative conditioning reveals

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left(M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z)-M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt=\sum_{j=1}^{9}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Z_{k}^{j}(z^{\prime}\psi_{n}^{\gamma},z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})-\mathbb{E}\left[\left.Z_{k}^{j}(z^{\prime}\psi_{n}^{\gamma},z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})\right|\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sum_{j=1}^{9}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{k}^{j}(z^{\prime}\psi_{n}^{\gamma},z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})^{2}\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}\psi_{n}^{2}\frac{1}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(T_{0})|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}n^{-(1-\gamma/2)},\end{split}

(C.4)

where the second last step applied Lemma
C.2
. Next, we apply a chaining with variance bounds, i.e. with the Young-Orlicz function
\psi(x)=x^{2}
. To this aim, we define

\rho_{n}(z,z^{\prime}):=Cn^{-1/2+\gamma/4}|z-z^{\prime}|,

where
C^{2}
equals the constant on the right-hand side of (
C.4
), ensuring the moment bound
\mathbb{E}[(M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z)-M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z^{\prime}))^{2}]\leq\rho_{n}(z,z^{\prime})^{2}
. Denoting

D(u,\mathcal{T},\rho_{n}):=\max\{\#\mathcal{T}_{0}:\mathcal{T}_{0}\subset\mathcal{T},\rho_{n}(t,t^{\prime})\geq u\textrm{ for different }t,t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{0}\},

then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{0\leq z\leq K}\left|M_{n,\gamma}^{1}(z)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{CKn^{-1/2+\gamma/4}}\sqrt{D(u,[0,K],\rho_{n})}du

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{CKn^{-1/2+\gamma/4}}\sqrt{CKn^{-1/2+\gamma/4}u^{-1}}du

\displaystyle=2CKn^{-1/2+\gamma/4}\longrightarrow 0.

\bullet\ \mathbf{M_{n,\gamma}^{2}}
.

In order to prove
\sup_{0\leq z\leq K}|M_{n,\gamma}(z)^{2}|\rightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0
, we will establish that its expectation

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}+\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}\right|\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\right)\right]

converges to zero. In order to bound this term we first use
\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}

which yields

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}\right|\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}\right|\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\right).

By the upper bound (
B.1
) on the transition density we obtain the moment bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}\}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}\exp\left(-\frac{(\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-y)^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)dy\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{(\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)\right]\leq\frac{C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}{\sqrt{k}},

which then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{2,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}\right|\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}Kn^{-1/2}\rightarrow 0.

Next, we use the inequality

\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}<\overline{X}^{c}_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\geq\rho_{0}\}}.

Then again by applying Corollary (
B.1
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}<\overline{X}^{c}_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\geq\rho_{0}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\geq\rho_{0}\}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}\exp\left(-\frac{(\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-y)^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)dy\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{(\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0}-K\psi_{n}^{\gamma})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}{\sqrt{k}}+C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-x_{0})^{2}}{2k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}}\right)dy

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}{\sqrt{k}}.

Consequently, we also obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathbbm{1}_{I_{8,k}^{0,z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}}\right|\overline{X}^{c}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}/\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\rceil}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}Kn^{-1/2}\rightarrow 0

and the proof is complete.

∎

In the next result, we establish that the rates in Theorem
2.1
and Proposition
3.3
are indeed minimax optimal. Note that the proof is quite short which is due to the fact that it makes use of the drift expansion provided by Lemma
C.1
which itself is a highly non-trivial result.

Proposition C.3
.

Let
\gamma\in[0,1)
. Then the estimation rate
\psi_{n}^{\gamma}=n^{-(1+\gamma)/2}
for
\rho
is minimax optimal, i.e. for any
s>0
we have

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\inf_{T_{n}^{\rho}}\sup_{\rho}\mathbb{P}_{\rho,\alpha,\beta}\left(n^{(1+\gamma)/2}\left|T_{n}^{\rho}-\rho\right|>s\right)>0,

where the infimum is taken over all estimators
T_{n}^{\rho}
based on
X_{0},X_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},\dots,X_{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
.

By Theorem
2.2
and
(2.9)
in
Tsybakov 2009
it is enough to prove that the Kullback divergence
D_{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}},\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}+2s\psi_{n}^{\gamma},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}})
remains bounded as
n\to\infty
. Using Lemma
C.1
and Corollary
B.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle D_{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}},\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}+2s\psi_{n}^{\gamma},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt=\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left[\log\left(\frac{\prod_{k=1}^{n}p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}{\prod_{k=1}^{n}p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+2s\psi_{n}^{\gamma},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt=-\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left[\left.\log\left(\frac{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+2s\psi_{n}^{\gamma},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}\right)\right|X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq 2s\max\{F_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}},\tilde{F}_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left[\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\Lambda_{n,\gamma}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left((X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})_{k=0,1,\dots,n}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left[r_{n,\gamma}(1,2s\psi_{n}^{\gamma})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}s\frac{\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)\right]+C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}s^{2}\frac{(\psi_{n}^{\gamma})^{2}\sqrt{n}}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(s)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

which remains bounded as
n\to\infty
.
∎

D
Proof of (2A) for
\gamma\in(0,1)

In this section, the tightness in (2A) is proven for the case
\gamma\in(0,1)
. As compared to
Brutsche and Rohde 2026
which covers the high-frequency regime
\gamma=1
, the innovation consists of describing typical path properties of
(X_{t})_{0\leq t\leq n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
via self-similarity and coupling that establishes a connection between the high-frequency regime and that for all other
\gamma\in(0,1)
. An instructive example of this was already given in the important Lemma
5.1
in the main paper. In the same spirit, we have the following preliminary result.

Lemma D.1
.

Let
\epsilon>0
,
0\leq L<K
and
X
be a solution of (
1.1
) with
\sigma_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}
and
X_{0}=x_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
\eta=\eta_{\epsilon}>0
independent of
L,K
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}_{0}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+Ln^{-\gamma/2}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}+Kn^{-\gamma/2}\}}>\left(K-L\right)\beta_{0}^{-2}\eta\right)>1-\epsilon.

Assume that
X
is started in
x_{0}
and recall the coupling time
T_{\rho_{0}}^{x_{0}}
given in (
3.4
). Then, we have for the coupled process
\overline{X}^{c}
defined in (
3.5
) the distributional identity

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+Ln^{-\gamma/2}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}+Kn^{-\gamma/2}\}}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{-1}\rceil}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+Ln^{-\gamma/2}<\overline{X}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c}<\rho_{0}+Kn^{-\gamma/2}\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+Ln^{-\gamma/2}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}}<\rho_{0}+Kn^{-\gamma/2}\}}\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+Ln^{-\gamma/2}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}}<\rho_{0}+Kn^{-\gamma/2}\}}\end{split}

(D.1)

As
\mathbb{P}(T_{\rho_{0}}^{x_{0}}<\infty)=1
, there exists
T_{0}>0
such that
\mathbb{P}(T_{\rho_{0}}^{x_{0}}<T_{0})>1-\epsilon/4
. Using the upper bound of the transition density (
B.1
), we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{-1}\rceil}\hskip-8.5359pt\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+Ln^{-\gamma/2}<\overline{X}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c}<\rho_{0}+Kn^{-\gamma/2}\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+Ln^{-\gamma/2}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}}<\rho_{0}+Kn^{-\gamma/2}\}}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{-1}\rceil}\int_{Ln^{-\gamma/2}}^{Kn^{-\gamma/2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\exp\left(-\frac{\min\{(y-x_{0})^{2},(y-\rho_{0})^{2}\}}{2\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)dy

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}}{\sqrt{n}}(K-L)n^{-\gamma/2}(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil T_{0}(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{-1}\rceil}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(K-L)(T_{0}+1)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}},

which converges to zero, i.e. the first summand in (
D.1
) converges stochastically to zero and it suffices to prove that

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+Ln^{-\gamma/2}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}}<\rho_{0}+Kn^{-\gamma/2}\}}>2(K-L)\beta_{0}^{-2}\eta\right)>1-\epsilon/4.

Applying the self-similarity of Lemma
3.1
, we have for an OBM
Y
started in
Y_{0}=\rho_{0}
with diffusion coefficient
\sigma_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}
that this is a consequence of

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+L/\sqrt{n}<Y_{(k-1)/n}<\rho_{0}+K/\sqrt{n}\}}>2(K-L)\beta_{0}^{-2}\eta\right)>1-\epsilon/4,

which follows from Lemma
3.2
in
Brutsche and Rohde 2026
applied to the function
\mathbbm{1}_{[L,K]}
for
n
sufficiently large and the fact that for any
\epsilon>0
we can choose
\eta
small enough such that
\mathbb{P}(L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(Y)>4\eta)>1-\epsilon/4
as seen in the proof of Lemma
5.1
by continuity of measures and the fact that
\mathbb{P}(L_{1}^{\rho_{0}}(Y)>0)=1
. Note in particular that this choice of
\eta
does not depend on
K
and
L
.
∎

We now turn to the proof of (2A). Here, we first introduce three events that describe typical behavior of the process
(X_{t})_{0\leq t\leq n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
. Let
\epsilon>0
.

•

Define

\displaystyle A_{1}(n):=\left\{-\Gamma\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\inf_{0\leq t\leq n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\overline{X}_{t}^{c},\sup_{0\leq t\leq n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\overline{X}_{t}^{c}<\Gamma\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right\}.

(D.2)

As by Jensen’s inequality, the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality and Itô’s isometry,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}|\overline{X}_{t}^{c}|\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\sigma_{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\overline{X}_{s}^{c})dW_{s}\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},

there exists a constant
\Gamma>0
such that for large enough
n
we have the bound
\mathbb{P}_{0}(A_{1}(n))>1-\epsilon
.

•

We define the event

\displaystyle A_{3}(n)=\left\{\inf_{y\in[\rho_{0}-\zeta\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},\rho_{0}+\zeta\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}]}L_{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{y}(\overline{X}^{c})>\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\xi\right\}.

(D.3)

Proving that
\zeta
and
\xi
can be chosen such that
\mathbb{P}(A_{3}(n))>1-\epsilon
is stated in Lemma
5.1
.

•

Motivated by the Hölder-continuity of each sampling path for any order strictly less than
1/2
, we define the event

\displaystyle A_{4}(n):=\left\{\sup_{|t-s|<\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}|\overline{X}_{t}^{c}-\overline{X}_{s}^{c}|\leq(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9}\right\}.

(D.4)

Applying Markov’s inequality and Theorem
1
in
Fischer and Nappo 2010
yields existence of a constant
\tilde{C}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{|t-s|<\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}|\overline{X}_{t}^{c}-\overline{X}_{s}^{c}|>(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{4/9}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{-4/9}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{|t-s|<\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}|\overline{X}_{t}^{c}-\overline{X}_{s}^{c}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}(\Delta_{n}^{\gamma})^{1/18}\sqrt{\log(2n)}\longrightarrow 0.

From this, we deduce that there exists
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathbb{P}(A_{4}(n))>1-\epsilon
for all
n\geq n_{0}
.

We omit an event
A_{2}
that has no corresponding analogue in the high-frequency proof and is somehow absorbed into
A_{3}(n)
. This makes it easier to compare the proofs for different sampling schemes. As a first step towards (2A), we prove

\displaystyle\left|\hat{\rho}_{n}-\rho_{0}\right|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(n^{-\gamma/2}\right).

(D.5)

To this aim, recall the normalized log-likelihood
\ell_{n}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)
given in (
3.1
) and fix an arbitrary
\epsilon>0
. As
\ell_{n}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(0)=0
and
n^{(1+\gamma)/2}(\hat{\rho}_{n}-\rho_{0})\in\mathrm{Argsup}_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\ell_{n}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(zn^{-(1+\gamma)/2})
by definition, the property
|\hat{\rho}_{n}-\rho_{0}|>Kn^{-\gamma/2}
implies
\sup_{|\theta|>Kn^{-\gamma/2}}\ell_{n}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)\geq 0
. Consequently, we are going to show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{K\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(n^{\gamma/2}|\hat{\rho}_{n}-\rho_{0}|>K\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\limsup_{K\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{n^{\gamma/2}|\theta|>K}\ell_{n,\gamma}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta)\geq 0\right)<\epsilon,

where both cases
\theta<0
and
\theta\geq 0
can be dealt with analogously and we only discuss the second one. The crucial observation is that
\ell_{n}(\theta_{\rho})
can be decomposed into a dominant term that and a remainder that can be bounded independently of
\theta_{\rho}
. The precise statement is given in Lemma
D.2
and requires the definition of

\displaystyle N_{n}^{L}(\theta_{\rho})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left[\log\left(\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}}\right)-\frac{(\overline{X}_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c}-\overline{X}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c})^{2}}{2\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\beta_{0}^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}+Ln^{-\gamma/2}\leq\overline{X}_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{c}<\rho_{0}+\theta_{\rho}\}},

where the parameter
L
will be specified later. Then, we have the following result:

Lemma D.2
.

Let
K,L\geq 0
,
\epsilon>0
and
\Theta_{n}^{1}:=[Kn^{-\gamma/2},n^{1/4-\gamma/2}]
,
\Theta_{n}^{2}:=(n^{1/4-\gamma/2},\infty)
. Then there exists a sequence of sets
(A_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\mathbb{P}_{0}(A_{n}^{c})\leq\epsilon
for
n\geq n_{0}
, such that for
i=1,2
,

\sup_{\theta_{\rho}\in\Theta_{n}^{i}}\ell_{n,\gamma}^{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(\theta_{\rho})\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}\leq\sup_{\theta_{\rho}\in\Theta_{n}^{i}}N_{n}^{L}(\theta_{\rho})\mathbbm{1}_{A_{n}}+F_{n}^{i}(K,L),

where
F_{n}^{i}(K,L)\geq 0
and the moment bounds
\mathbb{E}[F_{n}^{1}(K,L)n^{-1/2}]\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(L)<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}[F_{n}^{2}(K,L)n^{-1/2-\gamma/6}]\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(L)<\infty
with a constant
C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(L)
independent of
K
and
n
hold true.

The proof can be done analogously to that of Lemma
3.1
in
Brutsche and Rohde 2026
. The only difference is that all steps have to be conducted for the stepsize
\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}
instead of
1/n
and the sets
A_{1},A_{3}
and
A_{4}
in the proof have to be replaced by the suitable modified versions given in (
D.2
), (
D.3
) and (
D.4
). Moreover, the set

\left\{\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}L_{1}^{y}(Y)\leq\xi_{u}\right\}

for an OBM
Y
started in
\rho_{0}
has to be replaced by

A_{2}(n):=\left\{\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}L_{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{y}(\overline{X}^{c})\leq\xi_{u}\sqrt{n\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right\}.

Note that for any
\epsilon>0
there exists
\xi_{u}>0
such that
\mathbb{P}(A_{2}(n))>1-\epsilon
for
n
large enough, which can be deduce by the fact that
\mathbb{P}(\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}L_{1}^{y}(Y)>\xi_{u})>1-\epsilon
, analogously as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

Note that
\Theta_{n}^{1}
and
\Theta_{n}^{2}
in Lemma
D.2
are disjoint and
\Theta_{n}^{1}\cup\Theta_{n}^{2}=\{\theta:\ \theta\geq Kn^{-\gamma/2}\}
. Moreover, the moment bound on
F_{n}^{1}
of order
n^{1/2}
provided by this result is optimal, whereas the order
n^{1/2+\gamma/6}
for
F_{n}^{2}
is not optimal, but sufficiently accurate for our purpose. To proceed, recall the sets
A_{1}(n),A_{3}(n)
and
A_{4}(n)
given in (
D.2
)-(
D.4
) and let the constants
\xi,\zeta,\Gamma
be specified in such a way that the bounds of their respective probabilities are given for
\epsilon/6
(instead of
\epsilon
). Additionally, we introduce the event

A_{5}(n):=\left\{|F_{n}^{1}(K,L)|\leq C_{F}\sqrt{n},\ |F_{n}^{2}(K,L)|\leq C_{F}n^{1/2+\gamma/6}\right\},

where
C_{F}>0
is chosen large enough such that
\mathbb{P}(A_{5}(n))>1-\epsilon/6
which is possible by Lemma
D.2
and Markov’s inequality. Last but not least, let
A_{6}(n)
be the set from Lemma
D.1
with corresponding choice of
\eta
such that
\mathbb{P}(A_{6}(n)^{c})<\epsilon/6
. Finally, we introduce the event we will be working on during the proof of (
D.5
) as

\displaystyle A(n)=A_{1}(n)\cap A_{3}(n)\cap A_{4}(n)\cap A_{5}(n)\cap A_{6}(n)\cap A_{7}(n),

(D.6)

where
A_{7}(n)
is the set
A_{n}
in Lemma
D.2
for
\epsilon/6
. Then by the findings above we have for
n\geq n_{0}
large enough that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A(n)^{c})\leq\sum_{j=1,3,4,5,6,7}\mathbb{P}(A_{j}(n)^{c})\leq\epsilon.

The proof of (
D.5
) then follows by arguing line-by-line as from
(4.6)
in
Brutsche and Rohde 2026
onwards, including Subsection
4.2
to extend from
n^{\gamma/2}
- to
n^{(1+\gamma)/2}
-consistency. When proving the analogue of Lemma
4.3
, the application of Lemma
3.2
is is replaced by the same self-similarity and coupling argument used in the proof of Lemma
D.1
.

E
Proof of Proposition
3.4

We introduce the sets

\displaystyle J_{1,k}(\rho):=\left\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho,X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho\right\},

\displaystyle J_{2,k}(\rho):=\left\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\geq\rho,X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}>\rho\right\},

\displaystyle J_{3,k}(\rho):=\left\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho<X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right\},

\displaystyle J_{4,k}(\rho):=\left\{X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho\leq X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right\}.

We abbreviate
\psi_{n}^{\gamma}=n^{-(1+\gamma)/2}
and set
\alpha_{n}=\alpha_{0}+\theta_{\alpha}/\sqrt{n}
,
\beta_{n}=\beta_{0}+\theta_{\beta}/\sqrt{n}
. Using these events, we can estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in[-K,K]}\sup_{-L\leq\theta_{\alpha},\theta_{\beta}\leq L}\left|\ell_{n,\gamma}^{3}\left(zn^{-(1+\gamma)/2},\theta_{\alpha}/\sqrt{n},\theta_{\beta}/\sqrt{n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq\sum_{m=1}^{4}\sup_{z\in[-K,K]}\sup_{|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|,|\beta_{n}-\beta_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\left|T_{n}^{m}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})\right|

where

\displaystyle T_{n}^{m}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\log\left(\frac{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{m,k}(\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt-\left.\log\left(\frac{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0},\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}{p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{m,k}(\rho_{0})}\right)

We now prove stochastic convergence

\sup_{z\in[-K,K]}\sup_{|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|,|\beta_{n}-\beta_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\left|T_{n}^{m}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})\right|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}0

for
m=1,2,3,4
. Here, we will only provide details for
m=1,3
as
m=2
follows along the lines of
m=1
and
m=4
is analogous to
m=3
. Moreover, we only treat the supremum over
z\in[0,K]
as the argumens for
z\in[-K,0]
are the same.

\bullet\ \underline{m=1}:
By explicitly plugging in the transition density (
2.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle T_{n}^{1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})

\displaystyle=T_{n}^{1,1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})+T_{n}^{1,2}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})+T_{n}^{1,3}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}),

where

\displaystyle T_{n}^{1,1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\log\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\alpha_{n}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})}-\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\right),

\displaystyle T_{n}^{1,2}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\alpha_{n}^{2}}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})}-\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\right),

\displaystyle T_{n}^{1,3}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\log\left(\frac{1-\frac{\alpha_{n}-\beta_{n}}{\alpha_{n}+\beta_{n}}\exp\left(-\frac{2}{\alpha_{n}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0}-z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0}-z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})\right)}{1-\frac{\alpha_{0}-\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\beta_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{2}{\alpha_{0}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0}-z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0}-z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})\right)}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\log\left(\frac{1-\frac{\alpha_{n}-\beta_{n}}{\alpha_{n}+\beta_{n}}\exp\left(-\frac{2}{\alpha_{n}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})\right)}{1-\frac{\alpha_{0}-\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\beta_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{2}{\alpha_{0}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})\right)}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}.

Subsequently, we are going to prove

\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\sup_{|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|,|\beta_{n}-\beta_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\left|T_{n}^{1,j}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})\right|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}_{0}}0

for
j=1,2,3
. For the first two cases, we note that

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})}-\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0},\rho_{0}<X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0},\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})}-\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}<X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}.\end{split}

(E.1)

Then, using the upper bound (
B.1
) on the transition density gives for
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}<X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}\right]\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-x_{0})^{2}}{2(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}}\right)dy\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}=C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\frac{1}{\sqrt{kn}}\end{split}

(E.2)

and the same bound holds when replacing
X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
with
X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}
.

\mathbf{-\ T_{n}^{1,1}}
.

First, we observe that by a Taylor expansion,

\left|\log(\alpha_{0}/\alpha_{n})\right|\leq C_{\alpha_{0}}|\alpha_{0}-\alpha_{n}|\leq C_{\alpha_{0}}L/\sqrt{n}.

Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\sup_{|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|,|\beta_{n}-\beta_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\left|T_{n}^{1,1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{C_{\alpha_{0}}L}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})}-\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{C_{\alpha_{0}}L}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}<X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}<\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}LK\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\longrightarrow 0.

\mathbf{-\ T_{n}^{1,2}}
.

First of all, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\frac{(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\left.\frac{(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right|X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}\gamma}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}\right]\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\frac{1}{\sqrt{kn}},\end{split}

(E.3)

where the last step uses the bound (
E.2
) and Corollary
B.2
.
Moreover, applying iterative conditioning, the bound (
B.1
) on the transition density and using boundedness of
x\mapsto x^{2}\exp(-x^{2}/c)
yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\frac{(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho_{0}\leq X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}\}}\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}\}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right.\\
&\hskip 199.16928pt\left.\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{2\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)dy\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{\{X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}\}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{0}+K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{4\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)dy\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}K\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{4\max\{\alpha_{0}^{2},\beta_{0}^{2}\}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)\right]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}K\frac{1}{\sqrt{nk}},\end{split}

(E.4)

where the last step uses Corollary
B.1
. Finally, (
E.1
), a direct evaluation of
|\alpha_{0}^{-2}-\alpha_{n}^{-2}|
and the above two estimates reveal

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\sup_{z\in[-K,K]}\sup_{|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|,|\beta_{n}-\beta_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\left|T_{n}^{1,2}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})\right|\right]\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}LK\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\rightarrow 0.

\mathbf{-\ T_{n}^{1,3}}
.

We abbreviate

\displaystyle a_{N}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\alpha_{N}-\beta_{N}}{\alpha_{N}+\beta_{N}},

\displaystyle b_{N,k}(z)

\displaystyle:=\exp\left(-\frac{2}{\alpha_{N}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\left(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0}-z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\right)\left(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0}-z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\right)\right).

for
N\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
. Note that
b_{N,k}(z)
also depends on the sample size
n
and
\gamma
which is suppressed in the notation. With these abbreviations, we can split

T_{n}^{1,3}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})=S_{n}^{1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})+S_{n}^{2}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{n}^{1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})&:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left[\log\left(\frac{1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(z)}{1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(z)}\right)-\log\left(\frac{1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(0)}{1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(0)}\right)\right]\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})},\\
S_{n}^{2}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})&:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\log\left(\frac{1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(z)}{1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(z)}\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0}+z\psi_{n}^{\gamma})}-\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\right).\end{split}

(E.5)

We will discuss these terms seperately.

\mathbf{\blacktriangleright S_{n}^{1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})}
. Using standard properties of the logarithm, one finds

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(z)}{1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(z)}\right)-\log\left(\frac{1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(0)}{1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(0)}\right)=\log\left(\frac{(1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(z))(1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(0))}{(1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(z))(1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(0))}\right)

On the event
J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})
, we have
b_{N,k}(z)\leq 1
, meaning that for
N\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,

\left(1-\frac{|\alpha_{N}-\beta_{N}|}{\alpha_{N}+\beta_{N}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\leq\left(1-a_{N}b_{N,k}(z)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\leq\left(1+\frac{|\alpha_{N}-\beta_{N}|}{\alpha_{N}+\beta_{N}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}.

As
\alpha_{n}\to\alpha_{0}
,
\beta_{n}\rightarrow\beta_{0}
, we conclude that there exists a constant
C=C(\alpha_{0},\beta_{0})>0
such that

C^{-1}\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\leq\frac{(1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(z))(1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(0))}{(1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(z))(1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(0))}\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\leq C\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}.

As
x/(1+x)\leq\log(1+x)\leq x
for every
x>-1
, we can therefore conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\log\left(\frac{1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(z)}{1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(z)}\right)-\log\left(\frac{1-a_{n}b_{n,k}(0)}{1-a_{0}b_{0,k}(0)}\right)\right|\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\left|a_{0}b_{0,k}(z)-a_{n}b_{n,k}(z)+a_{n}b_{n,k}(0)-a_{0}b_{0,k}(0)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt\left.+a_{0}a_{n}(b_{n,k}(z)b_{0,k}(0)-b_{0,k}(z)b_{n,k}(0))\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\left(S_{n,k}^{1,1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})+S_{n,k}^{1,2}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})+S_{n,k}^{1,3}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})\right),

with

\displaystyle S_{n,k}^{1,1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left|(a_{0}-a_{n})(b_{0,k}(z)-b_{0,k}(0)\right|\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})},

\displaystyle S_{n,k}^{1,2}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left|a_{n}\left(b_{0,k}(z)-b_{0,k}(0)-b_{n,k}(z)+b_{n,k}(0)\right)\right|\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})},

\displaystyle S_{n,k}^{1,3}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left|a_{0}a_{n}(b_{n,k}(z)b_{0,k}(0)-b_{0,k}(z)b_{n,k}(0))\right|\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}.

\mathbf{-\ S_{n,k}^{1,1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})}
. Using a Taylor expansion, we find

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|,|\beta_{n}-\beta_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\left|a_{0}-a_{n}\right|\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt=\sup_{|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|,|\beta_{n}-\beta_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\left|\frac{\alpha_{0}-\beta_{0}}{\alpha_{0}+\beta_{0}}-\frac{\alpha_{n}-\beta_{n}}{\alpha_{n}+\beta_{n}}\right|\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(L)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}.\end{split}

(E.6)

To proceed, we first observe that

\displaystyle\left|b_{0,k}(z)-b_{0,k}(0)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\exp\left(-\frac{2}{\alpha_{0}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\cdot\left|\exp\left(\frac{2}{\alpha_{0}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}+X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-2\rho_{0})-\frac{2}{\alpha_{0}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}z^{2}(\psi_{n}^{\gamma})^{2}\right)-1\right|.

As
X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\leq\rho_{0}
on
J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})
, we obtain using
1-e^{-x}\leq x
,

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\left|b_{0,k}(z)-b_{0,k}(0)\right|\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0}}(K)\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\exp\left(-\frac{2}{\alpha_{0}^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\left(n^{-(1-\gamma)/2}\left|X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}+X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-2\rho_{0}\right|+\frac{1}{n}\right).

Moreover, the explicit form of the transition density given in (
2.1
) reveals the bound

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}p_{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}^{\rho_{0}}(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}},X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\alpha^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\left(1+\frac{|\alpha-\beta|}{\alpha+\beta}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right).

Using this explicit bound of the transition density on the event
J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})
, the upper bound
(1+x)\exp(-x^{2}/c)\leq C_{c}\exp(-x^{2}/(2c)
in the second inequality and
(a+b)^{2}\geq a^{2}+b^{2}
for
ab\geq 0
in the third one gives the bound

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\left.\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\left|b_{0,k}(z)-b_{0,k}(0)\right|\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\right|X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\int_{-\infty}^{\rho_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}(y-\rho_{0})(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\left(n^{-(1-\gamma)/2}\left|y+X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-2\rho_{0}\right|+\frac{1}{n}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)dy\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt=C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\int_{-\infty}^{\rho_{0}}\left(n^{-(1-\gamma)/2}\left|y+X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-2\rho_{0}\right|+\frac{1}{n}\right)\\
&\hskip 142.26378pt\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-2\rho_{0}+X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{2\alpha^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)dy\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}n^{-(1-\gamma)/2}\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\int_{-\infty}^{\rho_{0}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-2\rho_{0}+X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}})^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)dy\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}n^{-1/2}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)\int_{-\infty}^{\rho_{0}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}}\exp\left(-\frac{(y-\rho_{0})^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right)dy\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}n^{-1/2}\exp\left(-\frac{(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0})^{2}}{4\alpha^{2}\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\right).\end{split}

(E.7)

Taking expectation and using Corollary
B.1
and (
E.6
) finally reveals

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\sup_{|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|,|\beta_{n}-\beta_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\left|S_{n,k}^{1,1}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})\right|\right]\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}(L)\frac{1}{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}.

(E.8)

\mathbf{-\ S_{n,k}^{1,2}(z,\alpha_{n},\beta_{n})}
. We introduce the abbreviation

\displaystyle d(z):=\frac{2}{\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}\left(X_{k\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0}-z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\right)\left(X_{(k-1)\Delta_{n}^{\gamma}}-\rho_{0}-z\psi_{n}^{\gamma}\right)\geq 0,

(E.9)

where the inequality is valid on
J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})
which we are working on. By a second order Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&b_{n,k}(z)-b_{0,k}(z)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha_{n}^{2}}d(z)\right)-\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}d(z)\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt=\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\alpha_{n}^{2}}\right)d(z)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}d(z)\right)+\frac{1}{2}\exp\left(-\xi(z)d(z)\right)d(z)^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\alpha_{n}^{2}}\right)^{2},\end{split}

(E.10)

where
\xi(z)
is some intermediate value between
\alpha_{n}^{-2}
and
\alpha_{0}^{-2}
. Subsequently, let
n
be large enough such that
\alpha_{0}/2\leq\xi(z)\leq 3/2\alpha_{0}
uniformly in
z
. This entails that

\exp\left(-\xi(z)d(z)\right)d(z)^{2}\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}d(z)\right)d(z)^{2}\leq C_{\alpha_{0}}\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}d(z)\right).

To proceed, note that
d(z)\geq d(0)
on
J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\exp\left(-\xi(z)d(z)\right)d(z)^{2}\right]&\leq C_{\alpha_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}d(0)\right)\right]\leq C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\end{split}

(E.11)

by an analogous derivation as in (
E.7
) with a subsequent application of Corollary
B.1
. In total we obtain for the remainder

\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\sup_{|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\exp\left(-\xi(z)d(z)\right)d(z)^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\alpha_{n}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{C_{\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}}L^{2}}{n\sqrt{k}}.

The same reasoning can be applied to
b_{n,k}(0)-b_{0,k}(0)
. Additionally, we have the estimate
|\alpha_{n}^{-2}-\alpha_{0}^{-2}|\leq C_{\alpha_{0}}L/\sqrt{n}
for
|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}
, such that we obtain in total

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\left[\sup_{z\in[0,K]}\sup_{|\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{0}|\leq L/\sqrt{n}}\mathbbm{1}_{J_{1,k}(\rho_{0})}\left|b_{0,k}(z)-b_{0,k}(0)-b_{n,k}(z)+b_{n,k}(0)\right|\right]\\
